## Bounded material-fact pass

9. **One finite pass, pre-declared and then closed.** The bounded-research rule permits a single material-fact pass for genuinely disputed points or for a short pre-declared list whose omission would create a real syllabus gap. The list was fixed at five items before the pass began, and every one of them was resolved **inside the permitted source base** without external research.
   - **Item one, the Hallisalasya evidence.** The 2026 route depends on a sentence that does not appear in the painting chapter. It was located in the same core source at PDF p. 63, in the architecture chapter's Bagh Caves entry, and is quoted in full in Lesson 5. Resolved internally.
   - **Item two, the Bagh date conflict.** The two ranges were confirmed as both present in the same source, at PDF p. 63 and PDF p. 323. Reported as two, not resolved.
   - **Item three, the Ajanta technique and pigment statements.** Confirmed at PDF p. 58 for the rock-cut tradition's dry-surface method and six colours, and in the painting chapter for Ajanta's fresco secco and its five named pigments. Resolved internally.
   - **Item four, whether Badami carries a described painting programme.** Searched across the architecture chapter and the painting chapter. **Result: nil.** Recorded as a disclosed boundary rather than filled.
   - **Item five, the exact 2026 Preliminary wording and item number.** Resolved from the official local paper, with the numbering discrepancy disclosed above. This is PYQ verification rather than research.
10. **No external factual research was conducted.** No optional source, exhaustive corroboration or decorative current affairs was pursued, and the pass was closed after these five items. Where the bounded base leaves a point unresolved - the two Bagh ranges, the Sittanavasal technique word, the *Shangri Ramayana* location, the 2026 item number, Sansar Chand's dual role - the point is recorded as open uncertainty in Lesson 19's contested list rather than settled.

## Current-affairs source used

11. **Lesson 19, the single current linkage.** Search run: "Ajanta paintings restoration State Museum Hyderabad eye copies 2026". Item used: *Deccan Chronicle*, "Ajanta Paintings at State Museum to Get New Life", **published 28 July 2026**, read directly, with the publication date confirmed from the article's own structured metadata. Recorded content used: that the Ajanta paintings at the State Museum are set to be restored in collaboration with the Noor International Microfilm Centre and the heritage department; that the Ajanta Paintings Gallery houses hand-painted replicas known as "eye copies" of the Ajanta murals, described in the report as making the State Museum the only museum in India to possess such a collection; that the paintings were made on canvas by artists specially sent to Ajanta, with around forty on display and the rest preserved in store; that they depict the life of the Buddha, Bodhisattvas, Jataka tales, royal courts, merchants, musicians, dancers and everyday life, including replicas of Padmapani Bodhisattva, Vajrapani Bodhisattva and several courtly scenes; that many have suffered damage and that experts will begin restoration after completing an ongoing manuscript conservation project; the quoted statement of Prof. Arjun Rao, director of the heritage department, on stabilising and restoring the paintings using traditional methods; and the record that the Hyderabad archaeological department, under Dr Ghulam Yazdani, initiated the replica project in the early twentieth century before the originals deteriorated further. The item is attributed to that dated newspaper report of a museum and department position; no primary conservation document was opened, and the item asserts nothing about the present condition of the original Ajanta murals or about conservation policy.
12. **No other current item is used anywhere in this session.** Eighteen of the nineteen lessons record truthfully in their pre-teach checklists that no current linkage is required.

## Evidence, discrepancy and prohibition controls

- **Disclosed source discrepancy one - the Bagh excavation dates.** The same source gives a 5th-6th century range at one location and a 5th-7th century range at another, both qualified as mainly Gupta. Both are stated in Lesson 5 and in the contested list; neither is chosen.
- **Disclosed source discrepancy two - the Sittanavasal technique word.** The source names fresco secco explicitly for Ajanta and Ellora, but writes only "fresco technique with many mineral colours" for Sittanavasal in a sentence grouping it with Ajanta and Bagh. Both statements are reported in Lesson 3 and neither is extended.
- **Disclosed source discrepancy three - the *Shangri Ramayana*.** The same source places it both as produced in Jammu itself and as created by Pahari artists in Kullu. Both are stated in Lesson 15 and in the contested list; they are not reconciled.
- **Disclosed source discrepancy four - Sansar Chand's role.** He is named among the three greatest painters of the Pahari School, as the patron under whom Kangra reached its zenith, and as the most important patron of Himachal wall painting. All three are reported as recorded; no single role is asserted.
- **Disclosed ledger-versus-paper discrepancy - the 2026 item number.** Recorded in the PYQ section and included among the twenty entries in the Lesson 19 contested list. Neither number is asserted, and the official numbering is not decisively established within the evidence held here.
- **Disclosed coverage boundary - Badami.** Named in the mural site list with no described painting programme anywhere in the permitted source base. Lesson 3 states the boundary, Lesson 19 repeats it, and no Badami painting subject, date, cave number or technique appears anywhere in this session. This is recorded as a gap in the evidence, not a gap in coverage.
- **Disclosed coverage boundary - the Deccani stream.** Named as one of three medieval styles with four corroborating mentions and one characterisation, and with no named court, painter, manuscript, date or technique. Lesson 13 teaches the boundary as itself examinable and supplies the safe formulations; nothing is supplied from memory.
- **Disclosed evidence limit - three clipped source tables.** The illustrated-manuscript details column, the Company-versus-Bazaar comparison's right-hand cells, and three artist names in the notable-paintings table are clipped in the available record. What is legible is reproduced; **no clipped entry is completed anywhere**, and Lesson 17 names the three partial artist names explicitly as prohibited completions.
- **Attribution prohibitions observed absolutely.** *Bani Thani* is written as associated with Nihal Chand and is never assigned to another school. No painting anywhere in this session is paired with an artist beyond the pairs the record supplies. No Deccani painter is named. No individual Armamalai panel is assigned to the Jain or the Shaiva phase. The Hallisalasya dance is never assigned to a named Bagh cave or equated with a named modern classical dance form.
- **Hedges preserved.** "Said to have patronised" for Babur and Bihzad; "it is said" for Shah Jahan's European inspiration; "probably" for the Tanjore origin; "said to provide a link" for Sittanavasal between Ajanta and Thanjavur; "seen as the progenitor" for the Lepakshi-Kalamkari relation; "believed" for Madhubani's Ramayana-era origin and for Ravan Chhaya's hunting-lodge function; "some art historians argue" for Rabindranath Tagore's paintings and his writings; "supposed to have arisen" for the Bengal School's origin; and "said to have existed since" for the seventh-century start of Tamil wall painting.
- **Numerical prohibitions observed absolutely.** No painting-level date, pigment recipe, panel dimension, cost figure, practitioner count or museum accession detail is stated anywhere. The only measurements written are those the record supplies: twenty-five square inches and one-sixth for the miniature rule; 642 shelters, nearly 400 marked, 29 species and 16 shades at Bhimbetka; 17 rock beds at *Ezhadippattam*; 312 *sadhanas*; more than 300 square feet at Lepakshi; 70 pillars in its Rang Mandapa; 22 princely states in the Pahari family; 15-30 feet for Phad and up to 45 feet for Cheriyal; and around 40 replicas on display in the dated current item.
- **Status-claim discipline.** Institutional recognition is asserted only where the record supplies it with a date or as a plain statement of fact: Bhimbetka's World Heritage status in 2003; the 1970 recognition of Jagdamba Devi; Cheriyal's geographical-indication status in 2007; Mysore Ganjifa's in 2008; and the geographical-indication status recorded for Tanjore, Madhubani and Kalamkari without a year. Every current registry, conservation, display or institutional position is routed for verification and is not asserted from this session.
- **Boundary discipline.** Building history, excavation sequence and structural description are routed to the architecture topics. Carved images, attribute doctrine and the Chola bronze are routed to sculpture and iconography. Textual transmission of the illustrated works is routed to languages and manuscripts. Dye and print trades are routed to crafts and textiles. Protection law, institutions and changeable status are routed to heritage. Swadeshi politics is routed to modern history. Living-community conditions are routed to society.
- **Learner-facing register.** Generation, verification and research-pass language is confined to this ledger. In the lessons, the same limits appear as ordinary evidence statements - what is recorded, what is not recorded, and what may not be supplied.
- **Bounded-research discipline.** The substantive base is the complete canonical Basic owner, the complete canonical Advanced owner and one core OCR art source. External work was limited to exact PYQ verification from locally held official papers and ledgers, one pre-declared five-item material-fact pass resolved entirely inside the permitted source base, and one brief dated current linkage, after which the pass was closed.
